```
# Chores-09.md

General chores notes — design captures (forward-looking) and
post-implementation chore entries. Same shape as chores-01..08.md;
09 starts here because chores-07 (0.42.0 cycle, 600+ lines) and
chores-08 (0.41.1 cycle, 1500+ lines) are both already large; the
init-clone-refactor rebase landing is a natural new-file
boundary.

Subsection headers use the trailing-version format from CLAUDE.md
when they correspond to a release: `## Description (X.Y.Z)`.

## init-clone-refactor rebase landing (0.42.0-4.7)

Rebased main (0.42.0-0..-4.6) onto init-clone-refactor at its
0.41.1 close-out tip (`slvprlpw`). Chids preserved; ochid
trailers in the rebased commits stayed structurally valid (the
.claude pairings they point at had been WC-only during the icr
divergence; -4.7 itself re-establishes the .claude side).

### What needed real work

- **ttzwvpoq (0.42.0-1):** full `Scope` enum lifted wholesale
  from pre-rebase main; `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `is_bot_only`
  and `Single` / `parse_scope` since icr's bundle design keeps
  them dormant.
- **mvusyowm (0.42.0-2):** icr's bundle-flatten `InitArgs` +
  `ScopeOption`/`ScopeKind` already supersedes mvusyowm's
  parser-switch + `Option<Scope>` design; took icr wholesale
  across all 5 init.rs conflict regions. The cycle's narrative
  intent (custom `--scope` parser accepting paths) becomes
  dormant on the icr base; the `Scope::Single` variant survives
  for future use.
- **kyurxpnu (0.42.0-4.5):** CLAUDE.md substep references merged
  (lulqxovr's icr-side decomposition pointer + kyurxpnu's
  external `substep-protocol.md` / `jj-revsets.md` links) into
  a single continuation paragraph under `### Versioning`.
- **Reference renumbering** — cycle's `[72]`/`[73]`/`[74]`
  collided with icr's chores-08 anchors: `[72]→[76]`,
  `[73]→[77]`, `[74]→[78]`, plus self-refs in chores-07's
  -4.5 and -4.6 sections.

### Precautions taken (most unnecessary in retrospect)

- `main-2` local duplicate bookmark — never read.
- `gca-icr-main` common-ancestor marker — never referenced.
- `rslv-commit` cursor bookmark — handy as a navigation
  aid during cascade, not load-bearing (`@-` works).
- `../vc-x1-main` + `../vc-x1-icr` reference clones — useful
  for content lookup at specific chids; replaceable with
  `jj log -r <chid> --patch` against the local repo.
- Filesystem snapshots `vc-x1-20260505-1`, `vc-x1-20260506-1`
  — never restored from.
- `~/vc-x1-rebase-status.md` scratch file — useful for
  resuming across sessions, but ~200 lines was over-detailed;
  a 30-line cursor + decisions log would have sufficed.

### What was load-bearing

- `jj op restore` for local rewinds.
...
```

506	## Ops layer architecture (forward-looking)
507	
508	Design target for subsequent cycles: separate clap-aware
509	parsing from subcommand operation logic so future front-ends
510	(e.g. a TUI for bot-conversation exploration, or library
511	embedding) call the same core without retrofitting. Captures
512	the conclusion of the 0.42.0-4.7 side discussion about
513	`args.account.account` ergonomics — renaming / accessor
514	shortcuts were rejected as hiding the architecture mismatch
515	rather than fixing it.
516	
517	### Goals
518	
519	- Subcommand bodies access flat fields (`opts.account`), not
520	  nested clap shapes (`args.account.account`).
521	- `src/options_flags/` leaves remain the single source of
522	  truth for flag definition (parser, help, completer).
523	- Per-subcommand `-h` stays bundle-specific.
524	- Tab completion (`vc-x1 init <TAB>`, `--account=<TAB>`)
525	  keeps working via clap_complete + dynamic completers.
526	- The ops layer is callable without clap as a dependency
527	  (today's `plan_init(args: &InitArgs, ...)` is not).
528	
529	### Two layers, three structures per subcommand
530	
531	- **CLI layer (clap-aware):** `InitArgs` / `CloneArgs` / etc.
532	  `#[derive(Args)]` types that flatten leaves from
533	  `src/options_flags/`. Own clap metadata for `-h` and
534	  completion. Live at the binary edge.
535	- **Ops layer (clap-free):** `InitOptions` / `CloneOptions`
536	  — plain structs, flat fields, `Default`. Plus `Workspace`,
537	  the shared platform handle (workspace root, loaded
538	  `UserConfig`, optional progress sink). Entrypoints are
539	  `ops::init(ws: &Workspace, opts: &InitOptions) -> Result<InitOutcome, InitError>`.
540	- **Boundary conversion:** `From<&InitArgs> for InitOptions`
541	  — one contained site per subcommand where leaf nesting is
542	  unpacked. Op bodies never see `args.xxx.xxx`.
543	
544	### `Workspace` ("context") vs per-op options
545	
546	- **`Workspace`:** shared platform every op runs against —
547	  workspace root, loaded user config, optional progress
548	  sink. Same shape across subcommands.
549	- **`InitOptions`** etc.: per-op input; shape differs by
550	  subcommand.
551	
552	Two parameters, not a single merged "god context." The
553	signature `fn init(ws: &Workspace, opts: &InitOptions)`
554	documents what the op depends on; a merged `Context`
555	containing every possible field would let any op silently
556	read any field with no signature-level visibility.
557	
558	If `Workspace` itself grows, escape valves (defer until
559	needed):
560	
561	- Split into `Workspace` (paths + config, read-only, cheap)
562	  vs `Session` (progress / cancellation, mutable). Most ops
563	  want `&Workspace`; long-running ops also accept
564	  `&Session`.
565	- Trait-based DI (`fn init<W: HasConfig + HasFs>(...)`) for
566	  multiple front-ends with genuinely different platform
567	  surfaces. Heavy in Rust; not the default.
568	
569	### Five rules for the ops layer
570	
571	1. **Plain options, flat fields, `Default`.** No clap types
572	   in `InitOptions`. Domain values like `RepoSelector` are
573	   fine (they're domain types); leaf wrappers like
574	   `RepoOption` are not.
575	2. **Typed errors.** `enum InitError` (likely via
576	   `thiserror`), not `Box<dyn Error>`. A TUI matches
577	   variants to pick dialogs; CLI formats them.
578	   `Box<dyn Error>` discards exactly the information a GUI
579	   most needs.
580	3. **Returned outcomes, not `println!`.** Each op returns a
581	   structured result; CLI formats it for stdout, TUI
582	   populates panels. Library writes nothing to stdout
583	   itself.
584	4. **Progress via a sink.** Long-running ops accept an
585	   optional `&mut dyn ProgressSink` (or
586	   `mpsc::Sender<Event>`). CLI installs a stderr sink; TUI
587	   a status-bar sink; tests a recording sink. `log` /
588	   `tracing` covers diagnostics but is lossy for structured
589	   progress.
590	5. **No globals, no implicit cwd/env reads in ops.**
591	   Everything an op needs goes in `Workspace` or
592	   `*Options`. CLI resolves cwd/env once at startup and
593	   builds the handle. This is what lets a multi-window TUI
594	   drive multiple workspaces without cross-contamination.
595	
596	### Completion stays at the clap layer
597	
598	- Static structure (`-h`, subcommand layout, flag presence):
599	  clap derive on `InitArgs` + leaves. Unchanged from today.
600	- Dynamic value completion (`--account=<TAB>` against
601	  accounts in user config): `ArgValueCompleter` attached to
602	  the leaf in `src/options_flags/`. Already enabled via
603	  `clap_complete`'s `unstable-dynamic` feature. The leaf is
604	  the right home — completion is a clap-aware concern with
605	  no place in `InitOptions`.
606	
607	Ops layer stays clap-free; leaf layer stays clap-aware.
608	Completion drives nothing in the ops layer.
609	
610	### The contained wart
611	
612	`From<&InitArgs> for InitOptions` walks leaf nesting once
613	per subcommand:
614	
615	```rust
616	impl From<&InitArgs> for InitOptions {
617	    fn from(a: &InitArgs) -> Self {
618	        Self {
619	            target: a.target.clone(),
620	            account: a.account.account.clone(),
621	            scope: a.scope.scope,
622	            private: a.provision.private.private,
623	            // ...
624	        }
625	    }
626	}
627	```
628	
629	Verbose but contained: one site per subcommand. Adding a new
630	flag becomes a two-edit change (clap struct + options struct
631	+ one conversion line). Accepted price of decoupling; bodies
632	are flat thereafter.
633	
634	### What this is *not*
635	
636	- **Not a god `Context`.** Two parameters
637	  (`&Workspace`, `&XOptions`), not one merged blob.
638	- **Not premature trait-based DI.** Concrete `Workspace` +
639	  `*Options` structs until a second front-end forces
640	  generalization.
641	- **Not a crate split.** Same crate, separate modules
642	  (e.g. `src/ops/init.rs` vs CLI edge). Promote to a
643	  workspace only when a second consumer crate appears.
644	- **Not removing `src/options_flags/`.** Leaves stay; only
645	  their consumers change shape.
646	
647	### Migration sketch
648	
649	The bot thinks the safest first move is converting one
650	subcommand end-to-end as the worked example before any
651	sweep. `init` is the largest surface; `sync` or `chid` would
652	be a lighter proof of concept.
653	
654	1. Introduce `ops::Workspace` (paths + `UserConfig` only; no
655	   progress sink yet). Wire it into one subcommand.
656	2. Introduce `XOptions` for the chosen first subcommand;
657	   write `From<&XArgs> for XOptions`; port the body to
658	   `&Workspace, &XOptions`.
659	3. Add typed errors + returned outcome for that subcommand.
660	4. Sweep remaining subcommands; each is a contained step.
661	5. (Later) introduce `ProgressSink` when a TUI need actually
662	   surfaces, or a long-running op wants structured progress.
663	
664	The bot thinks the right shape of `Workspace` matters more
665	than the right shape of `InitError` — defer error-type and
666	outcome-type design until step 1 has shaken out.
667	
668	### Open questions
669	
670	- `Workspace` carries the progress sink from day one even
671	  with no consumer? The bot's guess is no — leave it out and
672	  add when a real long-running op forces it; adding later is
673	  mechanical.
674	- `XOptions` owns values (`String`) or borrows (`&str`)? The
675	  bot's guess is owns — simpler, matches the programmatic-
676	  caller path (build then call). Defer borrow optimization
677	  until benchmarks call for it.
678	- Crate split timing — defer until a second consumer crate
679	  appears.
680	
681	## InitParams implementation (0.44.0)
682	
683	Single-step cycle: introduce `Context` + `InitParams` and
684	port `init` to the new shape. Establishes the worked
685	example for the design captured in
686	[Ops layer architecture (forward-looking)](#ops-layer-architecture-forward-looking)
687	above; remaining subcommands defer to later cycles.
688	
689	### Naming differs from "Ops layer architecture" section above
690	
691	Written before this cycle, the design section uses
692	`Workspace` and `XOptions`. This cycle adopts:
693	
694	- `Workspace` → `Context`. Cargo owns "workspace"
695	  formally, and this codebase already uses "workspace"
696	  for the dual-repo project root (`find_workspace_root`).
697	  Doubly overloaded.
698	- `XOptions` → `XParams`. Avoids collision with Rust's
699	  `Option<T>` (`InitParams` fields are a mix of required
700	  and `Option<T>`-typed, which `Options` muddles).
701	  Doesn't collide with `src/options_flags/` either.
702	
703	The forward-looking design section is left as-is for
704	this cycle; once 0.44.0 lands and the worked example
705	is in tree, that section can be retitled / updated
706	during a future close-out so the codebase has one
707	naming.
708	
709	### Why init
710	
711	`args.account.account` ergonomics — the trigger for the
712	whole design — lives here; init also has the deepest
713	leaf nesting (account / repo / scope / private /
714	template / push retry). If the pattern survives init,
715	sync / chid / push / clone port trivially.
716	
717	### In scope
718	
719	- `src/context.rs` (new): `Context` struct holding the
720	  workspace root path + loaded `UserConfig`. Built once
721	  at startup.
722	- `src/init/params.rs` (new): `InitParams` flat plain
723	  struct (owns its values) + `impl From<&InitArgs> for
724	  InitParams`.
725	- `src/init.rs`: port `plan_init` and any other
726	  init-side `pub fn` entrypoints to
727	  `fn (&Context, &InitParams)`. Body reads
728	  `params.account` instead of `args.account.account`.
729	- `src/main.rs`: build `Context` once at startup; the
730	  init dispatch arm passes `(&ctx, &(&args).into())`.
731	- `src/init/tests.rs`: pure-unit tests construct
732	  `Context` + `InitParams` directly (no clap
733	  `try_parse_from` plumbing); integration paths still
734	  exercise the clap edge.
735	
736	### Out of scope (deferred)
737	
738	- Sweep across sync / chid / push / clone / etc.
739	  Each is its own later cycle.
740	- Per chores-09 "five rules": typed errors, returned
741	  outcomes (vs `println!`), `ProgressSink`. Keep this
742	  cycle to the structural split only.
743	- `Context` fields beyond workspace root + user
744	  config. Defer until a real consumer surfaces.
745	
746	### Pre-commit checklist
747	
748	Standard: `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --
749	-D warnings`, `cargo test`, `cargo install --path .
750	--locked`, retest.
751	
752	### Edits
753	
754	- `Cargo.toml`: bump 0.43.0 → 0.44.0.
755	- `src/context.rs` (new): `Context` struct holding
756	  `user_config: UserConfig` + `Context::load()` ctor;
757	  declared `mod context;` in `src/main.rs`.
758	- `src/init/params.rs` (new): `InitParams` flat struct
759	  + `impl From<&InitArgs> for InitParams` (production
760	  default sets `create_symlink: true`); declared
761	  `mod params; pub use params::InitParams;` in
762	  `src/init.rs`.
763	- `src/init.rs`: `pub fn init` signature changed
764	  from `init(args: &InitArgs, create_symlink: bool)`
765	  to `init(ctx: &Context, params: &InitParams)`
766	  (`create_symlink` folded into `InitParams`).
767	  Internal helpers (`plan_init`, `plan_from_url`,
768	  `plan_from_path`, `plan_from_bare_name`,
769	  `plan_remote`, `plan_local`, `build_plan`,
770	  `create_por`, `create_dual`, `push_repo`,
771	  `run_remote_step`) all switched from
772	  `args: &InitArgs` to `params: &InitParams`. Body
773	  reads `params.account` instead of
774	  `args.account.account` (and similarly across all
775	  leaf nestings). `args.config.resolve(ConfigKind::None)`

> AGENT

Acquainted. Here's where `vc-x1` stands.

**Project** — A Rust CLI (`vc-x1` v0.44.0, ~13.7k LOC) for managing a dual jj-git workspace: an app repo + a `.claude` bot-session repo, with synced commit messages (`ochid:` trailers), bookmark advancement, and a `push` command that wraps the commit→push→finalize flow with two approval gates. Subcommands: `init`, `clone`, `sync`, `chid`, `push`, `desc`/`fix-desc`/`validate-desc`, `list`, `show`, `finalize`, `symlink`.

**Repo state** — On `main` at `be7f2502` (0.44.0), working copy clean and empty. `.claude` has the usual live session-data churn. No in-progress multi-step cycle (`notes/todo.md > ## In Progress` is just the template stub).

**Last cycle (0.44.0)** — "Introduce Context + InitParams": the worked example for an *ops-layer decoupling* — separating clap-aware arg structs (`InitArgs`) from clap-free op input (`InitParams`, flat owned fields) plus a shared `Context` (workspace root + loaded `UserConfig`). Boundary conversion via `From<&InitArgs> for InitParams`. Goal: future front-ends (TUI, library embedding) call the core without clap. Naming note: the forward-looking design in `chores-09.md` says `Workspace`/`XOptions`; the implementation chose `Context`/`XParams` to dodge Cargo's "workspace" and Rust's `Option`.

**Next up (todo #1)** — Sweep the same `<Mod>Params` + `From<&XArgs>` pattern across the remaining subcommands (sync, chid, push, clone, desc, fix-desc, validate-desc, list, show, finalize, symlink) — each its own cycle. Other queued work: forks/multi-user + bot-data-formats follow-through, `--scope` sweep continuation (finalize, clone, validate-desc), symmetric `.vc-config.toml` schema, `test_helpers::Fixture` rename/migration, a `status`/`st` subcommand, `validate-repo` diagnostic.

What would you like to work on?

> DEVELOPER

Let's cleanup the terminology Context/XParams vs Workspace/XOptions in chores-09 and I'm thinking we squash that into 0.44.0, thoughts?

> AGENT

Here's the lay of the land before I touch anything.

## What the cleanup actually is

In `notes/chores-09.md`, two spots use the pre-0.44.0 names:

1. **`## Ops layer architecture (forward-looking)`** (lines ~506–679) — uses `Workspace` and `XOptions`/`InitOptions`/`CloneOptions` throughout, plus the subsection header ``### `Workspace` ("context") vs per-op options``, and the example signature `ops::init(ws: &Workspace, opts: &InitOptions)`. All of that becomes `Context` / `XParams` / `InitParams` / ``### `Context` vs per-op params`` / `init(ctx: &Context, params: &InitParams)`. The "Migration sketch" step "convert one subcommand end-to-end as the worked example" is now done (init, 0.44.0) — worth a one-line "✓ done in 0.44.0" note.

2. **`### Naming differs from "Ops layer architecture" section above`** (lines ~689–707) — once §1 is reconciled, this loses its reason to exist. I'd shrink it to a 2-line historical note keeping just the *why* (`Workspace`→`Context` because Cargo + `find_workspace_root` already own "workspace"; `Options`→`Params` to dodge `Option<T>`) and drop the "left as-is for this cycle / retitle during a future close-out" framing.

Scope check: other `Workspace*` hits in notes (`WorkspaceConfig`, `SingleRepoWorkspace`, jj-lib's `Workspace::load()` in chores-01, the `Workspace` handle in `bot-data-formats.md`) are unrelated concepts — leave them. `notes/todo.md` #1 already says "Context + InitParams", so it's fine.

## Squash into 0.44.0 vs a 0.44.1 doc cycle

**Status:** 0.44.0 is already pushed to `origin` in *both* repos (`main@origin` = `be7f2502` app / `c4a040a3` .claude). So this isn't a free local amend.

- **Squash into 0.44.0** — needs `jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .` + `jj git push --bookmark main -R .` (a force-push of already-published history), then clear `.vc-x1/push-state.toml` per the "out-of-band recovery" rule. Low-risk on a 2-day-old commit in a solo repo, but it's the exception path, not `vc-x1 push`. `.claude` wouldn't strictly need a force-push (change IDs survive squash, so ochid trailers stay valid) but you'd want a normal session commit there anyway. Also: chores-09's "Edits" list for 0.44.0 would need a small touch-up so it describes the squashed-in state.

- **0.44.1 doc-only cycle** — normal `vc-x1 push main`, two gates, no force-push, no state-file cleanup. One more version entry for a doc edit, but the project does that routinely (`0.21.1`, `0.22.1`, …). And chores-09 itself says the section "can be retitled / updated during a future close-out" — i.e. a follow-up was the anticipated path.

**My lean: 0.44.1.** The workflow is built around forward-only history, the notes already foreshadow a follow-up, and the force-push buys very little here. But it's close to a coin-flip — if you'd rather keep it folded in, the squash path is documented and safe in this case.

Which way do you want to go? (That decides whether step 1 is a version bump to `0.44.1` or a `--ignore-immutable` squash.)

```
//! Reusable CLI options and flags (OFs) — see

//! Pattern-A composition patterns and the Flag-vs-Option

/// Marker trait for pure-boolean bundles (every constituent leaf
/// is a Flag). See [Marker traits](README.md#marker-traits).
#[allow(dead_code, reason = "marker trait — see README.md#marker-traits")]
pub trait FlagBundle: clap::Args {}

pub trait OptionBundle: clap::Args {}

/// See [Marker traits](README.md#marker-traits).
#[allow(dead_code, reason = "marker trait — see README.md#marker-traits")]
pub trait OptionFlagBundle: clap::Args {}

/// value-parser. Conditional contract — implemented only when a
/// boolean leaf takes a value form (e.g. `--flag=true|false`).

/// directly. See [Marker traits](README.md#marker-traits).
#[allow(
    dead_code,
    reason = "conditional contract — see README.md#marker-traits"
)]

/// Banner string emitted as the first line of normal command runs
/// and shown at the top of subcommand `--help` output. Built from
/// `Cargo.toml`'s name + version at compile time so it stays in
/// sync with the bumped version.
const BANNER: &str = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));

/// Top-level about line — name, version, and the project tagline
/// on a single line. Used as the top-level `about` so `vc-x1 -h`
/// reads as one banner-plus-tagline header instead of two stacked
/// lines.
const TOP_ABOUT: &str = concat!(
    env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"),
    " ",
    env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
    " - jj workspace tooling"
);
```

> AGENT

Good call on a standalone doc cycle — and yeah, there's a real gap: `README.md` is user-facing usage, `src/options_flags/README.md` covers the leaf/bundle micro-pattern, `CLAUDE.md` is bot workflow — but nothing describes the app's *structure* (module map, the Args↔Context/Params split, subcommand dispatch, what's mid-migration). Worth its own document.

Here's how I'd shape it.

## Cycle
- **0.45.0, single-step, doc-only.** Focused scope → direct bump, one commit (per CLAUDE.md's single-step guidance). Normal `vc-x1 push main` flow.

## New doc: `notes/architecture.md`
Matches the existing `notes/` design-doc convention (`forks-multi-user.md`, `bot-data-formats.md`, `substep-protocol.md`). (Alternative: root `ARCHITECTURE.md` — GitHub special-cases that filename for discoverability. I lean `notes/` for consistency, but flag it as a choice.)

Proposed outline:
1. **Overview** — one binary, `vc-x1 <subcommand>`, jj dual-repo tooling.
2. **Module map** — `src/main.rs` (clap entry + dispatch + banner walker), `src/options_flags/` (reusable leaves/bundles → link its README), per-subcommand modules, `src/context.rs`, `src/scope.rs`, support modules (`repo_utils`, `toml_simple`, `url`, `logging`, test helpers).
3. **Two layers** — CLI layer (clap-aware `XArgs`, flatten leaves) ↔ ops layer (clap-free `Context` + `XParams`), boundary `From<&XArgs> for XParams`. This absorbs the *normative* content currently in chores-09's "Ops layer architecture (forward-looking)" — the five rules, `Context`-vs-per-op-params, "what this is not", completion-stays-at-clap-layer.
4. **Subcommand model** — dispatch pattern, `cli_with_banner` walker, completion (`clap_complete` + `ArgValueCompleter` on leaves).
5. **In-flight migrations + status tables** — *two* separate ones:
   - (a) **Args → Context/Params** (ops-layer decoupling): init ✓ (0.44.0); sync/chid/push/clone/desc/fix-desc/validate-desc/list/show/finalize/symlink ✗.
   - (b) **Per-subcommand inline flag structs → `src/options_flags/` leaves**: leaves exist today for account/config/dry_run/private/provision_bundle/push_retry/repo/scope/use_template (mostly init's surface); the rest still inline. (I'll verify the exact state when I write it.)
   - The doc explains *the pattern and why*; the **live checklist stays in `notes/todo.md` #1** — no duplicated TODO lists.
6. **Cross-links** — pointer added from `notes/README.md`, `src/options_flags/README.md`, and root `README.md`'s Contributing section.

## chores-09 reconciliation — decision point
The "Ops layer architecture (forward-looking)" section was forward-looking *because the doc didn't exist*. Now it will. Two ways to handle it:

- **(A) Trim to a stub** — replace the section body with "Superseded by `notes/architecture.md` (0.45.0); originally captured the ops-layer design pre-implementation," keeping maybe the "Open questions" if any still have residual value. chores files stay a per-cycle journal; the living spec lives in one place.
- **(B) Leave it as a journal entry, just fix terminology** — `Workspace`/`XOptions` → `Context`/`XParams` so it doesn't contradict reality, but keep the prose. Downside: it then duplicates architecture.md and the two can drift.

I lean **(A)** — single source of truth. Either way, the `### Naming differs from "Ops layer architecture" section above` subsection collapses to a one-liner (keep just the *why*: `Workspace`→`Context` because Cargo + `find_workspace_root` own "workspace"; `Options`→`Params` to dodge `Option<T>`) or disappears.

## Two things I'd want your call on before I start
1. **Doc location** — `notes/architecture.md` (my pick) vs root `ARCHITECTURE.md`?
2. **chores-09 section** — (A) trim to stub (my pick) vs (B) keep + just fix terminology?

Once you pick those I'll do the version bump to 0.45.0 and draft the doc.

```
151	    /// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes
152	    #[command(long_about = "Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.\n\n\
153	        Repo set is resolved (in order):\n  \
154	          - `-R` / `--repo`     exact list (back-compat / arbitrary multi-repo)\n  \
155	          - `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` dual-repo roles via `.vc-config.toml`\n  \
156	          - neither             default: `code,bot` when dual, else `code`\n\n\
157	        Default is --check (verify only) — fatal if any repo needs\n\
158	        action. Re-run with --no-check to apply (rebase / fast-forward).\n\
159	        Per repo:\n  \
160	          - up-to-date        nothing to do\n  \
161	          - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote\n  \
162	          - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)\n  \
163	          - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts\n  \
164	          - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip\n\n\
165	        Scripts and automation should pass --check or --no-check\n\
166	        explicitly — defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the\n\
167	        contract. Interactive use can rely on the default.\n\n\
168	        After the bookmark action, `@` is rebased onto the (possibly\n\
169	        advanced) bookmark if it isn't already a descendant, so trailing\n\
170	        working-copy writes (e.g. `.claude`'s `/exit` tail) don't end up\n\
171	        orphaned on a stale branch.\n\n\
172	        On any failure, every repo is reverted to its starting state via\n\
173	        `jj op restore`. Working-copy files are preserved across the\n\
174	        revert — the operation log rewinds but disk content stays.\n\n\
175	        Output shape:\n  \
176	          - all-up-to-date: one-line summary (`sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`)\n  \
177	          - action needed (--check):  per-repo fetch + state + fatal error\n  \

194	        single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a\n\
195	        state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can\n\
196	        resume without re-doing completed stages.\n\n\
197	        Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)\n\
198	        → message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →\n\
199	        commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both\n\
200	        → push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /\n\
201	        commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via\n\
202	        `jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.\n\
203	        After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and\n\
204	        recovery is forward-only.\n\n\
205	        Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes\n\
206	        to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body\n\
207	        across resumes so only the first invocation needs them."

212	fn run_command(result: Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>) -> ExitCode {
213	    match result {
214	        Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,
215	        Err(e) => {
216	            error!("{e}");
217	            ExitCode::FAILURE
218	        }
219	    }
220	}

222	/// Permanent sanity check for the `main`-bookmark tracking state
223	/// in both repos of the dual-repo workspace. Prints one line on
224	/// entry and one on exit of every command. If entry and exit
225	/// differ, the executing command is the culprit; if entry differs
226	/// from the previous command's exit, something *between*
227	/// invocations broke it. Originally added in 0.37.2 as a temporary
228	/// diagnostic; promoted to permanent in 0.37.4 after the user
229	/// reported "happens more than once" — the signal is worth keeping
230	/// in place until confidence in its correctness is well-established
231	/// across varied use, at which point a "silent when clean"
232	/// refinement would remove the steady-state noise without losing
233	/// detection value (see `notes/todo.md`).
234	///
235	/// Walks up from cwd to locate the workspace root (the directory
236	/// whose `.vc-config.toml` has `path = "/"`), then probes `<root>`
237	/// and `<root>/.claude`. Same labeling whether the user runs from
238	/// the app root, from `.claude`, or from any subdir.
239	fn bm_track(phase: &str, command_name: &str) {
240	    let header = format!("bm-track {phase} vc-x1 {command_name}");
241	    let root = match common::find_workspace_root() {
242	        Some(r) => r,
243	        None => {
244	            log::info!("{header}: no-workspace");
245	            return;
246	        }
247	    };
248	    let repos: [(std::path::PathBuf, &str); 2] =
249	        [(root.clone(), "app"), (root.join(".claude"), ".claude")];
250	    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
251	    for (repo, label) in repos {
252	        if !repo.join(".jj").exists() {
253	            parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=no-jj"));
254	            continue;
255	        }
256	        match bm_track_one(&repo, "main", "origin") {
257	            Ok(true) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=tracked")),
258	            Ok(false) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=NOT_TRACKED")),
259	            Err(e) => parts.push(format!(
260	                "{label}(main)=err({})",
261	                e.lines().next().unwrap_or("")
262	            )),
263	        }
264	    }
265	    log::info!("{header}: {}", parts.join(", "));
266	}
267	
268	/// Query jj for whether `bookmark` in `repo` is tracking `remote`.
269	/// Returns `Ok(true)` if the tracked-list entry for `bookmark` shows
270	/// an `@<remote>` line (in any form — `@origin:` when synced, or
271	/// `@origin (ahead by N commits):` / similar when divergent — both
272	/// still count as tracking). `Ok(false)` when no such line exists
273	/// (bookmark isn't tracking this remote or doesn't exist). `Err` on
274	/// subprocess failure.
275	fn bm_track_one(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str, remote: &str) -> Result<bool, String> {
276	    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();
277	    let output = std::process::Command::new("jj")
278	        .args(["bookmark", "list", "--tracked", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str])
279	        .output()
280	        .map_err(|e| format!("spawn: {e}"))?;
281	    if !output.status.success() {
282	        return Err(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string());
283	    }
284	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
285	    // Accept both the synced form (`@origin:`) and the decorated
286	    // divergent form (`@origin (ahead by N commits):`), which still
287	    // represents a tracking relationship.
288	    let colon = format!("@{remote}:");
289	    let paren = format!("@{remote} ");
290	    Ok(stdout.lines().any(|l| {
291	        let t = l.trim_start();
292	        t.starts_with(&colon) || t.starts_with(&paren)
293	    }))
294	}
295	
296	fn main() -> ExitCode {
297	    CompleteEnv::with_factory(cli_with_banner).complete();
298	    let matches = cli_with_banner().get_matches();
299	    let cli = match Cli::from_arg_matches(&matches) {
300	        Ok(cli) => cli,
301	        Err(e) => {
302	            e.exit();
303	        }
304	    };
305	
306	    let log_path = cli.log.as_ref().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().to_string());
307	    logging::CliLogger::init(cli.verbose, log_path.as_deref());
308	
309	    // The detached `finalize --exec` re-entry is the bot's
310	    // session-end child; it shouldn't print the banner (extra
311	    // chatter in its log) or surface user-facing failure markers
312	    // (those are meant for the user's next interactive run).
313	    let is_detached_exec = matches!(
314	        cli.command,
315	        Commands::Finalize(ref f) if f.exec
316	    );
317	
318	    // `-L` / `--no-label` on the read-only multi-repo subcommands
319	    // (chid, desc, list, show) makes their output script-parseable
320	    // — adding a leading `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` line would break that. Skip
321	    // the banner whenever the active subcommand has the flag set.
322	    let suppress_banner = match &cli.command {
323	        Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label,
324	        Commands::Desc(a) => a.common.no_label,
325	        Commands::List(a) => a.common.no_label,
326	        Commands::Show(a) => a.common.no_label,
327	        _ => false,
328	    };
329	
330	    if !is_detached_exec {
331	        if !suppress_banner {
332	            // Banner on every normal run, mirroring what `--help`
333	            // shows at the top.
334	            log::info!("{BANNER}");
335	        }
336	        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
337	    }
338	
339	    // Command name for bm-track output (first positional arg after
340	    // the binary; clap has already validated it by the time we get here).
341	    let command_name = std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| "?".to_string()); // OK: default when somehow invoked without a subcommand
342	
343	    if !is_detached_exec {
344	        bm_track("enter", &command_name);
345	    }
346	
347	    let exit_code = match cli.command {
348	        Commands::Chid(chid_args) => run_command(chid::chid(&chid_args)),
349	        Commands::Desc(desc_args) => run_command(desc::desc(&desc_args)),
350	        Commands::List(list_args) => run_command(list::list(&list_args)),
351	        Commands::Show(show_args) => run_command(show::show(&show_args)),
352	        Commands::ValidateDesc(validate_desc_args) => {
353	            run_command(validate_desc::validate_desc(&validate_desc_args))
354	        }
355	        Commands::FixDesc(fix_desc_args) => run_command(fix_desc::fix_desc(&fix_desc_args)),
356	        Commands::Clone(clone_args) => run_command(clone::clone_repo(&clone_args)),
357	        Commands::Init(init_args) => {
358	            let ctx = match context::Context::load() {
359	                Ok(c) => c,
360	                Err(e) => {
361	                    error!("{e}");
362	                    return ExitCode::FAILURE;
363	                }
364	            };
365	            let params = init::InitParams::from(&init_args);
366	            run_command(init::init(&ctx, &params))
367	        }
368	        Commands::Symlink(symlink_args) => run_command(symlink::symlink(&symlink_args)),
369	        Commands::Sync(sync_args) => run_command(sync::sync(&sync_args)),
370	        Commands::Finalize(finalize_args) => {
371	            let opts = match finalize_args.into_opts(cli.log) {
372	                Ok(opts) => opts,
373	                Err(e) => {
374	                    error!("{e}");
375	                    return ExitCode::FAILURE;
376	                }
377	            };
378	            run_command(finalize::finalize(&opts))
379	        }
380	        Commands::Push(push_args) => run_command(push::push(&push_args)),
381	    };
382	
383	    if !is_detached_exec {
384	        bm_track("exit ", &command_name);
385	    }
386	
387	    exit_code
388	}
389	
390	#[cfg(test)]
391	mod tests {
392	    use super::*;
393

397	            .unwrap_err()
398	            .to_string();
399	        assert!(err.contains("bogus"));
400	    }
401	}
```

```
/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.
fn run_retry(
    cmd: &str,
    args: &[&str],
    cwd: &Path,
    retry: &PushRetryOptions,
) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let mut last_err = String::new();
    for attempt in 1..=retry.push_retries {
        match run(cmd, args, cwd) {
            Ok(out) => {
                if attempt > 1 {
                    debug!("succeeded after {attempt} attempts");
                }
                return Ok(out);
            }
            Err(e) => {
                last_err = e.to_string();

=== chid.rs ===
use clap::Args;
use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
use log::{debug, info};

use crate::common;

#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct ChidArgs {
    #[command(flatten)]
    pub common: common::CommonArgs,
}

pub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    debug!("chid: enter");
    let c = &args.common;
    let spec = common::resolve_spec(c.pos_rev.as_deref(), c.pos_count, &c.revision, c.limit, "@");
    let hdr = common::resolve_header(&c.label, c.no_label);

    common::for_each_repo(&c.repos, &hdr, |workspace, repo| {
        let (ids, _) =
            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;

        for commit_id in &ids {
            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
            info!("{}", common::format_chid(&commit));
        }
        Ok(())
    })?;
    debug!("chid: exit");
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
```

1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	
9	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
10	database to better manage the information, TBD.
11	
12	Examples chore file:
13	```
14	# Chores-01.md
15	 
16	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
17	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
18	how and why of a particular chore.
19	
20	## Create a binary that lists jj info 
21	
22	This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
23	and using `jj-lib`
24	```
25	
26	## Workflow and conventions
27	
28	Bot-facing workflow, versioning, and code conventions live in
29	[`../CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md). Start there for:
30	
31	- **Versioning during development** — single-step vs multi-step,
32	  `-N` pre-release suffixes, done-marker discipline.
33	- **Code Conventions** — doc comments on every file / fn / method,
34	  `// OK: …` justifications on `unwrap*` calls, ask-on-ambiguity,
35	  stuck detection.
36	- **Commit-Push-Finalize Flow** — two-checkpoint per-step
37	  discipline with hard stop after finalize.
38	
39	## Todo format
40	
41	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
42	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
43	references.
44	
45	Todo items use lazy numbering — every entry begins with `1. ` and the
46	markdown renderer auto-numbers them. Reorder or insert items without
47	renumbering, and reference an entry by its displayed number ("let's
48	work on #3"). The Done section keeps `-` bullets — items aren't
49	referenced by number once completed.
50	
51	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
52	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
53	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
54	
55	Examples:
56	
57	# Todo
58	- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
59	- Fix bug Y [1]
60	
61	# Done
62	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
63	
64	[1]: bugs.md#bug-y
65	[2]: issues.md#issue-z
66	[3]: fixes.md#fix-z
67	
68	## Reference numbering
69	
70	Every note file (`todo.md`, `chores-NN.md`, `done.md`) keeps a
71	file-local `# References` section at the bottom. Reference numbers
72	are scoped to that file — `[1]` in `chores-07.md` and `[1]` in
73	`chores-01.md` are independent slots that may point at completely
74	different URLs. New chores files start their numbering at `[1]`.
75	
76	## Retiring Done entries
77	
78	`todo.md`'s `## Done` section is a rolling buffer of recently shipped
79	work, not a permanent log. Move entries into `done.md` at two natural
80	beats:
81	
82	- **Closing a ladder** — when the final `X.Y.Z` (no suffix) commit
83	  ships, decide which prior entries are no longer needed for nearby
84	  context and migrate them.
85	- **Opening a new ladder** — at `X.Y.Z-0`, do the same sweep before
86	  bumping the version.
87	
88	Migration mechanics:
89	
90	- Move the bullet itself from `todo.md > ## Done` to
91	  `done.md` (preserving the original ref number).
92	- Copy any references the moved entries cite into
93	  `done.md`'s `# References` section (those refs are file-local,
94	  so coexisting with `todo.md`'s namespace is fine).
95	- Prune any references in `todo.md > # References` no longer cited
96	  by anything in `## In Progress` / `## Todo` / `## Done`. This
97	  frees the numbers for future reuse.
98

```
1	# vc-x1
2	
3	- [Overview](#vc-x1)
4	- [Usage](#usage)
5	  - [Revision shortcuts](#revision-shortcuts)

19	
20	This is experiment 1 to explore creating a Vibe Coding (vc) environment.
21	We will investigate ways of using the dual jj-git repo concept, explored
22	in [hw-jjg-bot](https://github.com/winksaville/hw-jjg-bot.git) to
23	initially make it easy to see how the code base evolved. This is
24	made possible by the fact that we have two repos one with the code
25	and one with the conversation with the bot.
26	
27	I've chosen the jj-git environment because jj provides the concept that
28	each commit has an immutable changeID as well as the mutable commitID
29	of git. The idea is that each commit made on repo A writes the
30	changeID in the commit message to repo B. Thus there is a cross reference
31	between the two repos and this will allow vc-x1 to show how the repo
32	evolved and the entity (bot or human) can more clearly understand **how** and
33	most importantly **why** the code evolved.
34	
35	The solution space is wide open, from trivial CLI, web or app based
36	(mobile/non-mobile). In addition, I could see this as an extension to
37	existing programming editors like vscode and zed or even creating our
38	own IDE for vc.
39	
40	See [Initial commit with dual jj-git repos](./notes/chores-01.md#initial-commit-with-dual-jj-git-repos)
41	for how the initial commit was created with the dual jj-git repos. After
42	doing so and I then created this README.md file.
43
```

```
## Thoughts for the future

Forward-looking design discussion lives in
[`notes/forks-multi-user.md`](notes/forks-multi-user.md) —
forking the dual-repo workspace, multi-user collaboration,
multi-line `ochid:` trailers, bot-repo size and scaling
thresholds, URL-shaped per-user repos for distributed
projects. Treat as a design reference, not a status doc;
most of what's there is forward-looking, not yet
implemented.

## Contributing

Bot-following workflow, commit conventions, and code style are
canonical in [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md). Roughly in the order a
contributor encounters them:

- [Versioning](CLAUDE.md#versioning) — `-N` pre-release suffix
  convention (single-step vs multi-step); cross-links to
  Sub-step Workflow for finer depths.
- [Sub-step Workflow](CLAUDE.md#sub-step-workflow) — sub-step
  (`X.Y.Z-N.M`) / sub-sub-step (`X.Y.Z-N.M-K`) decomposition,
  per-sub-step cargo cycle, commit-first review model, ochid
  + `.claude` cadence, cycle close-out (squash vs keep
  separate), reviewing committed sub-steps.
- [Pre-commit Requirements](CLAUDE.md#pre-commit-requirements) —
  user approval gates, review-before-commit-block, per-file
  review checkpoints (with sub-step exception).
- [Pre-commit checklist](CLAUDE.md#pre-commit-checklist) — fmt
  / clippy / test / install + todo / chores / README updates.
- [Commit Message Style](CLAUDE.md#commit-message-style) —
  Conventional Commits + `(version)` suffix; body shape per
  app vs `.claude` repo.
- [ochid Trailers](CLAUDE.md#ochid-trailers) — paired
  cross-references between app and `.claude` commits.
- [Commit-Push-Finalize Flow](CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) —
  `vc-x1 push` two-gate ceremony (review + message), preflight,
  finalize-claude.
- [Code Conventions](CLAUDE.md#code-conventions) — doc comments
  on every file / fn / method, `// OK: …` on `unwrap*` calls,
  ask-on-ambiguity, stuck detection.

Task tracking and release details live under [notes/](notes/):
near-term tasks in [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md), per-release
details in `notes/chores-*.md`, and notes-specific formatting
rules in [notes/README.md](notes/README.md).
```

```
52	/// Build the clap command tree with `BANNER` set as `before_help`
53	/// on every subcommand (transitively). Top-level skips `before_help`
54	/// because its own `about` already carries the name+version+tagline.
55	/// Walks via `mut_subcommand` so individual subcommand
56	/// `#[command(long_about = ...)]` blocks don't have to repeat the
57	/// banner text.
58	fn cli_with_banner() -> clap::Command {
59	    fn add_to_subs(mut cmd: clap::Command) -> clap::Command {
60	        let names: Vec<String> = cmd
61	            .get_subcommands()
62	            .map(|c| c.get_name().to_string())
63	            .collect();
64	        for name in names {
65	            cmd = cmd.mut_subcommand(name, add_with_banner);
66	        }
67	        cmd
68	    }
69	    fn add_with_banner(mut cmd: clap::Command) -> clap::Command {
70	        cmd = cmd.before_help(BANNER);
71	        let names: Vec<String> = cmd
72	            .get_subcommands()
73	            .map(|c| c.get_name().to_string())
74	            .collect();
75	        for name in names {
76	            cmd = cmd.mut_subcommand(name, add_with_banner);
77	        }
78	        cmd
79	    }
80	    add_to_subs(Cli::command())
81	}
82	
83	#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
84	#[command(
85	    version,
86	    propagate_version = true,
87	    about = TOP_ABOUT,
88	    max_term_width = 80
89	)]
90	pub struct Cli {
91	    /// Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace
```

```
5://!   - `Roles(Vec<Side>)` — dual-repo workspace; the parsed
7://!   - `Single(PathBuf)` — explicit single-repo mode; ignores
11://! - Helpers (`has_code`, `is_both`, etc.) reflect the `Roles`
12://!   arm only; on `Single(_)` they all return `false`. Callers
34:/// - `Roles(_)` — dual-repo workspace; the requested side set.
35:/// - `Single(_)` — explicit single-repo mode at the given path.
37:pub enum Scope {
38:    Roles(Vec<Side>),
40:    Single(PathBuf),
48:///   → `Scope::Roles(...)`. Order is preserved.
50:///   → `Scope::Single(PathBuf)`. The raw string is stored; tilde
59:pub fn parse_scope(s: &str) -> Result<Scope, String> {
66:        return Ok(Scope::Single(PathBuf::from(s)));
69:        "code" => Ok(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code])),
70:        "bot" => Ok(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot])),
71:        "code,bot" => Ok(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot])),
72:        "bot,code" => Ok(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot, Side::Code])),
85:    /// True when the `Roles` arm includes the code side.
87:        matches!(self, Scope::Roles(v) if v.contains(&Side::Code))
90:    /// True when the `Roles` arm includes the bot side.
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/architecture.md",
  "content": "# Architecture\n\nHow the `vc-x1` binary is structured: the clap-aware CLI\nlayer, the (emerging) clap-free ops layer, the subcommand\nmodel, and the two in-flight migrations toward that shape.\n\nThis is a living document — when the migrations land, update\nthe status tables here. Per-cycle design discussion stays in\n`notes/chores-*.md`; the canonical \"current shape\" lives\nhere.\n\n## Overview\n\n`vc-x1` is a single binary, `vc-x1 <subcommand>`, for\nmanaging a jj-git **dual-repo workspace** — an app repo plus\na `.claude` bot-session repo whose commits cross-reference\neach other via `ochid:` trailers. See the root\n[`README.md`](../README.md) for the user-facing picture and\n[`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) for the bot workflow.\n\nEach subcommand is a module under `src/`. `src/main.rs` owns\nthe clap command tree and dispatch; everything else is a\nsubcommand module or a shared support module.\n\n## Module map\n\nCLI edge:\n\n- `src/main.rs` — `Cli` (`#[derive(Parser)]`) → `Commands`\n  (`#[derive(Subcommand)]`) → per-variant dispatch arms.\n  Also: `cli_with_banner()` (walks the command tree adding\n  the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as `before_help` on every\n  subcommand), `CompleteEnv` wiring for dynamic completion,\n  and `bm_track` (a permanent `main`-bookmark tracking\n  diagnostic printed on entry/exit of every command).\n- `src/options_flags/` — reusable CLI **leaves** and\n  **bundles** (`account`, `config`, `dry_run`, `private`,\n  `provision_bundle`, `push_retry`, `repo`, `scope`,\n  `use_template`) plus marker traits. The leaf/bundle\n  composition patterns and the Flag-vs-Option classification\n  are documented in\n  [`src/options_flags/README.md`](../src/options_flags/README.md).\n- `src/common.rs` — `CommonArgs` (shared positional\n  revision / `-R`,`--repo` list / `-n`,`--commits` /\n  `--limit` / `-L`,`--no-label`) flattened by the read-only\n  multi-repo subcommands (`chid`, `desc`, `list`, `show`),\n  plus the repo-iteration and revision-resolution helpers\n  they share.\n\nOps-layer scaffolding:\n\n- `src/context.rs` — `Context`: the shared platform handle\n  every op runs against. Today it carries the loaded\n  `UserConfig`; built once at startup via `Context::load()`.\n- `src/config.rs` — `UserConfig` (`~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`).\n- `src/scope.rs` — `Scope` enum (`Roles(Vec<Side>)` for the\n  dual-repo `code`/`bot` roles, `Single(PathBuf)` for\n  single-repo mode) and `parse_scope`.\n\nSubcommand modules — each holds an `XArgs` `#[derive(Args)]`\nstruct and a `pub fn x(...)` entrypoint:\n\n| Module | Subcommand | Notes |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `chid.rs` | `chid` | flattens `CommonArgs` |\n| `desc.rs` (+ `desc_helpers.rs`) | `desc` | flattens `CommonArgs` |\n| `list.rs` | `list` | flattens `CommonArgs` |\n| `show.rs` | `show` | flattens `CommonArgs` |\n| `validate_desc.rs` | `validate-desc` | |\n| `fix_desc.rs` | `fix-desc` | |\n| `clone.rs` | `clone` | |\n| `init.rs` (+ `init/params.rs`) | `init` | ops-layer worked example (0.44.0) |\n| `symlink.rs` | `symlink` | |\n| `sync.rs` | `sync` | |\n| `finalize.rs` | `finalize` | `FinalizeArgs::into_opts(log)` → `FinalizeOpts` |\n| `push.rs` | `push` | resumable state machine |\n\nSupport:\n\n- `src/repo_utils.rs`, `src/url.rs`, `src/toml_simple.rs`,\n  `src/logging.rs` — jj/repo helpers, URL parsing, a minimal\n  TOML reader, the CLI logger.\n- `src/test_helpers.rs`, `src/test_tmp_root.rs` —\n  `#[cfg(test)]` fixtures (`Fixture` / `FixturePor`) and\n  tempdir resolution. Subcommand modules also carry their\n  own `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (and `integration_tests` for\n  `sync` / `push`).\n\n## Subcommand model\n\nAdding a subcommand `x`:\n\n1. Create `src/x.rs` with `pub struct XArgs`\n   (`#[derive(Args)]`), composing leaves from\n   `src/options_flags/` where one exists.\n2. Add a variant `X(x::XArgs)` to `Commands` in\n   `src/main.rs` with a `///` summary (and\n   `#[command(long_about = …)]` for the long form).\n3. Add a dispatch arm in `main()` calling `x::x(&args)` (or,\n   once ported, `x::x(&ctx, &params)`).\n4. Doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive `--help`; add\n   `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, …)]` on any field whose doc\n   comment uses bullets (clap otherwise reflows them into\n   prose).\n5. Dynamic value completion (`--account=<TAB>`) is an\n   `ArgValueCompleter` attached to the leaf in\n   `src/options_flags/` — completion is a clap-aware concern\n   and stays at this layer.\n\n## Two layers: CLI args vs ops Context + Params\n\nGoal: separate clap-aware argument parsing from subcommand\noperation logic, so a future front-end (TUI, library\nembedding) can call the same core without dragging clap\nalong. The trigger was `args.account.account`-style\nnested-leaf access leaking into op bodies; accessor\nshortcuts were rejected as hiding the mismatch rather than\nfixing it.\n\n- **CLI layer (clap-aware)** — `XArgs` `#[derive(Args)]`\n  structs that flatten leaves from `src/options_flags/`. Own\n  the clap metadata for `--help` and completion. Live at the\n  binary edge in each subcommand module.\n- **Ops layer (clap-free)** — `Context` (shared platform:\n  workspace root, loaded `UserConfig`, later a progress\n  sink) plus a per-subcommand `XParams` plain struct (flat\n  fields, owns its values, `Default`). Entrypoint shape:\n  `fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XParams) -> Result<…>`.\n- **Boundary conversion** — `impl From<&XArgs> for XParams`,\n  one contained site per subcommand, where leaf nesting is\n  unpacked once. Op bodies read `params.account`, never\n  `args.account.account`.\n\nTwo parameters, not one merged \"god context\": the signature\ndocuments what the op depends on. `Context` is the same\nshape across subcommands; `XParams` differs per subcommand.\n\nRules for the ops layer (some still aspirational — see\n[Migration A](#migration-a--args--context--params)):\n\n1. Plain options, flat fields, `Default` — no clap types in\n   `XParams`. Domain types (`RepoSelector`, `Scope`) are\n   fine; leaf wrappers (`RepoOption`) are not.\n2. Typed errors (`enum XError`, not `Box<dyn Error>`) — a\n   TUI matches variants to pick dialogs; CLI formats them.\n3. Returned outcomes, not `println!` — each op returns a\n   structured result; CLI formats it, a library writes\n   nothing to stdout itself.\n4. Progress via an optional `&mut dyn ProgressSink` on\n   long-running ops — CLI installs a stderr sink, tests a\n   recording sink.\n5. No globals, no implicit cwd/env reads in ops — CLI\n   resolves cwd/env once at startup and builds `Context`.\n\nWhat this is **not**: not a god `Context` (two params, not\none blob); not premature trait-based DI (concrete structs\nuntil a second front-end forces it); not a crate split (same\ncrate, separate modules — promote to a workspace only when a\nsecond consumer crate appears); not removing\n`src/options_flags/` (leaves stay; only their consumers\nchange shape).\n\n### Naming\n\nEarlier drafts of this design (and the `notes/chores-09.md`\ncapture) used `Workspace` / `XOptions`. The implementation\n(0.44.0) chose `Context` / `XParams` instead:\n\n- `Workspace` → `Context` — Cargo owns \"workspace\" formally,\n  and this codebase already uses \"workspace\" for the\n  dual-repo project root (`find_workspace_root`).\n- `XOptions` → `XParams` — avoids visual collision with\n  `Option<T>` (a params struct mixes required and\n  `Option<T>`-typed fields) and with `src/options_flags/`.\n\n## Migration A — args → Context + Params\n\nPort each subcommand's `pub fn x(args: &XArgs)` to\n`pub fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XParams)`, adding an\n`XParams` flat struct + `impl From<&XArgs> for XParams` at\nthe binary edge. `init` was done as the worked example in\n0.44.0; the rest follow the same shape. The live checklist\nis the \"Ops layer / CLI decoupling\" item in\n[`todo.md`](todo.md).\n\n| Subcommand | Status |\n| --- | --- |\n| `init` | done (0.44.0) — `init(&Context, &InitParams)` |\n| `finalize` | partial — `FinalizeArgs::into_opts(log)` → `FinalizeOpts` (no `Context`; `into_opts` rather than `From`) |\n| `sync` | not started |\n| `chid` | not started |\n| `desc` | not started |\n| `list` | not started |\n| `show` | not started |\n| `validate-desc` | not started |\n| `fix-desc` | not started |\n| `clone` | not started |\n| `push` | not started |\n| `symlink` | not started |\n\nOut of scope for the early ports (deferred until a real\nconsumer surfaces): typed errors, returned outcomes vs\n`println!`, the `ProgressSink`, and any `Context` fields\nbeyond workspace root + `UserConfig`.\n\n## Migration B — per-subcommand flags → `src/options_flags/`\n\nIndependently of Migration A, per-subcommand inline `#[arg]`\nfields are being lifted into reusable leaves/bundles under\n`src/options_flags/` so a flag is defined (parser, help,\ncompleter) exactly once. See\n[`src/options_flags/README.md`](../src/options_flags/README.md)\nfor the leaf / bundle / Pattern-A mechanics and the\nFlag-vs-Option classification.\n\nState today:\n\n- **`init`** — fully composed from leaves/bundles\n  (`account`, `repo`, `scope`, the `provision` bundle,\n  `use_template`, `config`).\n- **`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`** — share\n  `common::CommonArgs`, a per-domain shared struct rather\n  than an `options_flags/` leaf. The \"CommonArgs sweep\"\n  todo item folds these into the leaf model (and drops the\n  repeatable `-R`/`--repo` in favor of the `--scope` path\n  form).\n- **`sync` / `push` / `clone` / `validate-desc` /\n  `fix-desc` / `finalize` / `symlink`** — still mostly\n  inline `#[arg]` fields. The `--scope` retrofits queued in\n  [`todo.md`](todo.md) are the usual entry point for\n  converting one.\n\n## See also\n\n- [`../README.md`](../README.md) — user-facing overview and\n  per-subcommand usage.\n- [`../CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) — bot workflow, versioning,\n  commit/push conventions, code conventions.\n- [`../src/options_flags/README.md`](../src/options_flags/README.md)\n  — leaf/bundle patterns for Migration B.\n- [`todo.md`](todo.md) — live task list, including both\n  migrations.\n- [`chores-09.md`](chores-09.md) — the 0.43.0 / 0.44.0\n  design capture this document supersedes.\n"
}
```

> AGENT

First unit done — `Cargo.toml` bumped to **0.45.0**, and **`notes/architecture.md`** written (new file). Structure:

- **Overview** — single binary, dual-repo model, pointers to README/CLAUDE.
- **Module map** — CLI edge (`main.rs`, `options_flags/`, `common.rs`), ops-layer scaffolding (`context.rs`, `config.rs`, `scope.rs`), a table of the 12 subcommand modules, support modules.
- **Subcommand model** — the 5-step "adding a subcommand" recipe.
- **Two layers** — CLI args ↔ ops `Context` + `XParams`, boundary `From<&XArgs>`, the five ops-layer rules (flagged as partly aspirational), "what this is not", and a `### Naming` subsection explaining the `Workspace`→`Context` / `XOptions`→`XParams` rename.
- **Migration A** (args → Context/Params) — status table: `init` done (0.44.0), `finalize` partial (`into_opts`), the other 10 not started; live checklist deferred to `todo.md`.
- **Migration B** (per-cmd flags → `options_flags/` leaves) — state: `init` fully leaf-composed, `chid`/`desc`/`list`/`show` share `CommonArgs`, the rest still inline `#[arg]`.
- **See also** — cross-links to README, CLAUDE, the OF README, todo, chores-09.

Two notes for your review:
1. I kept the `### Naming` rationale here (in the living doc) rather than chores-09 — when I trim chores-09 next, that subsection there collapses to a one-line pointer.
2. Module-map table lists production modules only; I mentioned test submodules in prose rather than cluttering the table. Say if you'd rather see them enumerated.

Once you've looked it over I'll move on to trimming chores-09.

No globals, no implicit cwd/env reads in ops — CLI\n   resolves cwd/env once at startup and builds `Context`.\n\nWhat this is **not**: not a god `Context` (two params, not\none blob); not premature trait-based DI (concrete structs\nuntil a second front-end forces it); not a crate split (same\ncrate, separate modules — promote to a workspace only when a\nsecond consumer crate appears); not removing\n`src/options_flags/` (leaves stay; only their consumers\nchange shape).\n\n### Naming\n\nEarlier drafts of this design (and the `notes/chores-09.md`\ncapture) used `Workspace` / `XOptions`. The implementation\n(0.44.0) chose `Context` / `XParams` instead:\n\n- `Workspace` → `Context` — Cargo owns \"workspace\" formally,\n  and this codebase already uses \"workspace\" for the\n  dual-repo project root (`find_workspace_root`).\n- `XOptions` → `XParams` — avoids visual collision with\n  `Option<T>` (a params struct mixes required and\n  `Option<T>`-typed fields) and with `src/options_flags/`.\n\n## Migration A — args → Context + Params\n\nPort each subcommand's `pub fn x(args: &XArgs)` to\n`pub fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XParams)`, adding an\n`XParams` flat struct + `impl From<&XArgs> for XParams` at\nthe binary edge. `init` was done as the worked example in\n0.44.0; the rest follow the same shape. The live checklist\nis the \"Ops layer / CLI decoupling\" item in\n[`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md).\n\n| Subcommand | Status |\n| --- | --- |\n| `init` | done (0.44.0) — `init(&Context, &InitParams)` |\n| `finalize` | partial — `FinalizeArgs::into_opts(log)` → `FinalizeOpts` (no `Context`; `into_opts` rather than `From`) |\n| `sync` | not started |\n| `chid` | not started |\n| `desc` | not started |\n| `list` | not started |\n| `show` | not started |\n| `validate-desc` | not started |\n| `fix-desc` | not started |\n| `clone` | not started |\n| `push` | not started |\n| `symlink` | not started |\n\nOut of scope for the early ports (deferred until a real\nconsumer surfaces): typed errors, returned outcomes vs\n`println!`, the `ProgressSink`, and any `Context` fields\nbeyond workspace root + `UserConfig`.\n\n## Migration B — per-subcommand flags → `src/options_flags/`\n\nIndependently of Migration A, per-subcommand inline `#[arg]`\nfields are being lifted into reusable leaves/bundles under\n`src/options_flags/` so a flag is defined (parser, help,\ncompleter) exactly once. See\n[`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md)\nfor the leaf / bundle / Pattern-A mechanics and the\nFlag-vs-Option classification.\n\nState today:\n\n- **`init`** — fully composed from leaves/bundles\n  (`account`, `repo`, `scope`, the `provision` bundle,\n  `use_template`, `config`).\n- **`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`** — share\n  `common::CommonArgs`, a per-domain shared struct rather\n  than an `options_flags/` leaf. The \"CommonArgs sweep\"\n  todo item folds these into the leaf model (and drops the\n  repeatable `-R`/`--repo` in favor of the `--scope` path\n  form).\n- **`sync` / `push` / `clone` / `validate-desc` /\n  `fix-desc` / `finalize` / `symlink`** — still mostly\n  inline `#[arg]` fields. The `--scope` retrofits queued in\n  [`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md) are the usual entry\n  point for converting one.\n\n## See also\n\n- [`README.md`](README.md) — user-facing overview and\n  per-subcommand usage.\n- [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) — bot workflow, versioning,\n  commit/push conventions, code conventions.\n- [`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md)\n  — leaf/bundle patterns for Migration B.\n- [`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md) — live task list,\n  including both migrations.\n- [`notes/chores-09.md`](notes/chores-09.md) — the\n  0.43.0 / 0.44.0 design capture this document supersedes.\n"
```json
}
```

> AGENT

Reviewed the edited `ARCHITECTURE.md`. The reorg (lead with the two-layer split, rename "ops layer" → "subcommand layer", restructure the module map into nested bullets, separate leaves vs bundles correctly) reads well. Here's what I'd tighten:

### Typos / wording
- **Line 6–9** (the "living document" para): `and and` → `and`; `continous` → `continuous`; `and time goes on` → `as time goes on`; and it's a run-on. Also `Discussions of "chores" reside in per-cycle design discussion in …` is doubled-up, and `notes/todo.md` isn't "chores". Suggest:
  > This is a living document; the app is under continuous development and the repo will keep changing. Per-cycle design discussion lives in `notes/chores-*.md`; near-term tasks in `notes/todo.md`.
- **Line 4**: "the (emerging) clap-free subcommand **model**" — should be "subcommand **layer**" to match the §"Two layers: CLI args vs subcommand Context + Params" heading. ("Subcommand model" is the separate §about wiring a new subcommand — two different things now share that word.)
- **Line 45**: `subcommads` → `subcommands`. Also the sentence is a fragment — "Two parameters in subcommands; the signature documents what the op depends on." reads better with a semicolon, and `op` → `subcommand` for consistency with the rename.
- **Line 82**: `CLI src/option_flags:` → `src/options_flags` (directory has the `s`).
- **Lines 83–84**: "This directory contains **every option for all subcommands**" overclaims — the Migration B section right below says most subcommands still have inline `#[arg]` fields, and `CommonArgs` lives in `common.rs`. Suggest: "Reusable options/flags shared across subcommands (Migration B tracks what's been moved here so far)."
- **Line 72**: `pub enum  Commands` (double space) and `clap SubCommand` → `clap Subcommand` (trait name); line 73 has trailing whitespace; line 153–154 has a double blank line before `### Naming`.

### Structure
- **`### Naming` (line 155)** is now a subsection of `## Subcommand model`, but it's about the `Workspace`→`Context` / `XOptions`→`XParams` rename — it belongs under `## Two layers` (or as its own `##`). Suggest moving it right after the "Two layers" rules list.
- **Dropped "What this is *not*" paragraph.** The original had: not a god `Context` (two params, not one blob); not premature trait-based DI; **not a crate split — promote to a workspace only when a second consumer crate appears**; not removing `src/options_flags/`. The crate-split point in particular is load-bearing guidance for a future contributor. I'd restore a trimmed version (one sentence). Your call — if you cut it for brevity, fine.
- Leading with "Two layers" before "Module map" means the reader meets `XArgs`/`Context`/`src/options_flags/` before the map says where they live. Defensible (it's the headline point) — just noting it; I wouldn't change it.

### Cross-doc consistency (needs a decision)
The doc now says **"subcommand layer"** everywhere, but:
- `notes/todo.md` #1 is titled **"Ops layer / CLI decoupling — sweep remaining subcommands"**
- `notes/chores-09.md` has **"## Ops layer architecture (forward-looking)"** and the 0.44.0 section references "the design captured in Ops layer architecture"

The doc's own Migration A section (line 175) already references the todo item as "Subcommand layer / CLI decoupling" — which doesn't match the actual title. Two ways to resolve:
1. **Rename to "subcommand layer" everywhere** — update todo.md #1's title and the chores-09 references when I trim it next. (My lean — "subcommand layer" is the clearer name and you've committed the doc to it.)
2. **Keep "ops layer" as the cross-doc term**, revert the doc's renaming.

### Accuracy — all good
Leaves (8: `account`/`config`/`dry_run`/`private`/`push_retry`/`repo`/`scope`/`use_template`) and the lone bundle (`provision_bundle`) match `src/options_flags/`. The subcommand table and Migration A/B status are accurate.

Want me to apply the typo/wording fixes + move `### Naming` + restore a one-line "what this is not"? And which way on the "ops layer" vs "subcommand layer" naming — rename everywhere?

```
1	//! Shared platform handle passed to every subcommand operation.
2	//!
3	//! `Context` holds platform state that is the same across every
4	//! subcommand: today, the loaded `UserConfig`. Built once at CLI
5	//! startup and threaded through to the op layer.
6	//!
7	//! The shape is deliberately minimal — fields like project root,
8	//! progress sinks, etc. are added when a real consumer surfaces.
9	//! See `notes/chores-09.md > ## Ops layer architecture
10	//! (forward-looking)` and `## InitParams implementation (0.44.0)`.
11	
12	use crate::config::{self, UserConfig};
13	
14	/// Shared platform handle for subcommand operations.
15	///
16	/// - `user_config`: the loaded user config
17	///   (`~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` or its discovered equivalent).
18	pub struct Context {
19	    pub user_config: UserConfig,
20	}
21	
22	impl Context {
23	    /// Build a `Context` by loading the user config from disk.
24	    pub fn load() -> Result<Self, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
25	        Ok(Self {
26	            user_config: config::load()?,
27	        })
28	    }
29	}
```

```
34	This division survives any future evolution. Anthropic

36	unchanged. New vendor (Cursor, Aider, custom in-house) →

39	
40	The bot thinks this is the load-bearing principle that
41	should govern every interaction between vc-x1 and bot data,
42	including future ops-layer / CLI decoupling work
43	(see `chores-09.md > Ops layer architecture`). The
44	`Workspace` handle there exposes paths and commits; it
45	does not expose parsed sessions.
46	
47	## Why dual-repo (merits-based defense)
48	
49	The dual-repo split (code repo + bot repo) originated
50	tactically — Claude Code stores sessions at
51	`~/.claude/projects/<path>/` by default, so symlinking
52	out to a sibling jj-git repo was the natural path of
53	least resistance. The split wasn't architectural at design
54	time. But it has earned its keep on its own merits, even
```

```
1	# Architecture
2	
3	How the `vc-x1` binary is structured: the clap-aware CLI
4	layer and the (emerging) clap-free subcommand layer.
5	
6	This is a living document; the app is under continuous
7	development and the repo will keep changing. Per-cycle design
8	discussion lives in `notes/chores-*.md`; near-term tasks in
9	`notes/todo.md`.
10	
11	## Overview
12	
13	`vc-x1` is a single binary that supports subcommands,
14	`vc-x1 <subcommand>`, for managing a jj-git **dual-repo workspace**
15	with an app repo plus a `.claude` bot-session repo. The commits
16	cross-reference each other via `ochid:` trailers at the bottom
17	of each commit message. See [`README.md`](README.md)
18	for the user-facing picture and [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) for
19	the bot workflow.
20	
21	## Two layers: CLI args vs subcommand Context + Params
22	
23	Goal: separate clap-aware argument parsing from subcommand
24	operation logic, so a future front-end (TUI, library
25	embedding) can call the same core without dragging clap
26	along. The trigger was `args.account.account`-style
27	nested-leaf access leaking into subcommand bodies; accessor
28	shortcuts were rejected as hiding the mismatch rather than
29	fixing it.
30	
31	- **CLI layer (clap-aware)** — `XArgs` `#[derive(Args)]`
32	  structs that flatten leaves from `src/options_flags/`. Own
33	  the clap metadata for `--help` and completion. Live at the
34	  binary edge in each subcommand module.
35	- **Subcommand layer (clap-free)** — `Context` (shared platform:
36	  workspace root, loaded `UserConfig`, later a progress
37	  sink) plus a per-subcommand `XParams` plain struct (flat
38	  fields, owns its values, `Default`). Entrypoint shape:
39	  `fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XParams) -> Result<…>`.
40	- **Boundary conversion** — `impl From<&XArgs> for XParams`,
41	  one contained site per subcommand, where leaf nesting is
42	  unpacked once. Subcommand bodies read `params.account`,
43	  never `args.account.account`.
44	
45	Two parameters per subcommand; the signature documents what
46	the subcommand depends on. `Context` is the same across all
47	subcommands; `XxxParams` carry the per-subcommand inputs.
48	
49	Rules for the subcommand layer (some still aspirational — see
50	[Migration A](#migration-a--args--context--params)):
51	
52	1. Plain options, flat fields, `Default` — no clap types in
53	   `XParams`. Domain types (`RepoSelector`, `Scope`) are
54	   fine; leaf wrappers (`RepoOption`) are not.
55	2. Typed errors (`enum XError`, not `Box<dyn Error>`) — a
56	   TUI matches variants to pick dialogs; CLI formats them.
57	3. Returned outcomes, not `println!` — each subcommand
58	   returns a structured result; CLI formats it, a library
59	   writes nothing to stdout itself.
60	4. Progress via an optional `&mut dyn ProgressSink` on
61	   long-running subcommands — CLI installs a stderr sink, tests a
62	   recording sink.
63	5. No globals, no implicit cwd/env reads in subcommands — CLI
64	   resolves cwd/env once at startup and builds `Context`.
65	
66	Not a god `Context` (two params, not one blob), not premature
67	trait-based DI (concrete structs until a second front-end
68	forces it), not a crate split (same crate, separate modules —
69	promote to a workspace only when a second consumer crate
70	appears), and not removing `src/options_flags/` (leaves stay;
71	only their consumers change shape).
72	
73	### Naming
74	
75	Earlier drafts of this design (and the `notes/chores-09.md`
76	capture) used `Workspace` / `XOptions`. The implementation
77	(0.44.0) chose `Context` / `XParams` instead:
78	
79	- `Workspace` → `Context` — Cargo owns "workspace" formally,
80	  and this codebase already uses "workspace" for the
81	  dual-repo project root (`find_workspace_root`).
82	- `XOptions` → `XParams` — avoids visual collision with
83	  `Option<T>` (a params struct mixes required and
84	  `Option<T>`-typed fields) and with `src/options_flags/`.
85	
86	## Module map
87	
88	CLI layer:
89	
90	- `src/main.rs` — the clap edge:
91	  - `pub struct Cli` — clap `Parser`.
92	  - `pub enum Commands` — clap `Subcommand`; matched in the
93	    dispatch arms.
94	 
95	- CLI functions:
96	  - `cli_with_banner()` (walks the command tree adding
97	  the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as `before_help` on every
98	  subcommand)
99	  - `CompleteEnv` wiring for dynamic completion
100	  - `bm_track` (a permanent `main`-bookmark tracking
101	  diagnostic printed on entry/exit of every command)
102	
103	- `src/options_flags/` — options/flags shared across
104	  subcommands, so a flag is defined once (parser, help,
105	  completer); Migration B tracks what's been moved here so
106	  far. See
107	  [`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md).
108	  - reusable **leaves**: `account`, `config`, `dry_run`,
109	    `private`, `push_retry`, `repo`, `scope`, `use_template`.
110	  - reusable **bundles**: `provision_bundle`.
111	- `src/common.rs` — `CommonArgs` (the shared positional-rev /
112	  `-R` repo-list / `-n` / `--limit` / `-L` flag set flattened
113	  by `chid`, `desc`, `list`, `show`) plus the repo-iteration
114	  and revision-resolution helpers they share.
115	
116	Subcommand layer scaffolding:
117	
118	- `src/context.rs` — `Context`: the shared platform handle
119	  every subcommand runs against. Today it carries the loaded
120	  `UserConfig`; built once at startup via `Context::load()`.
121	- `src/config.rs` — `UserConfig` (`~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`).
122	- `src/scope.rs` — `Scope` enum (`Roles(Vec<Side>)` for the
123	  dual-repo `code`/`bot` roles, `Single(PathBuf)` for
124	  single-repo mode) and `parse_scope`.
125	
126	Subcommand modules — each holds an `XArgs` `#[derive(Args)]`
127	struct and a `pub fn x(...)` entrypoint:
128	
129	| Module | Subcommand | Notes |
130	| --- | --- | --- |
131	| `chid.rs` | `chid` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
132	| `desc.rs` (+ `desc_helpers.rs`) | `desc` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
133	| `list.rs` | `list` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
134	| `show.rs` | `show` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
135	| `validate_desc.rs` | `validate-desc` | |
136	| `fix_desc.rs` | `fix-desc` | |
137	| `clone.rs` | `clone` | |
138	| `init.rs` (+ `init/params.rs`) | `init` | subcommand-layer worked example (0.44.0) |
139	| `symlink.rs` | `symlink` | |
140	| `sync.rs` | `sync` | |
141	| `finalize.rs` | `finalize` | `FinalizeArgs::into_opts(log)` → `FinalizeOpts` |
142	| `push.rs` | `push` | resumable state machine |
143	
144	Support:
145	
146	- `src/repo_utils.rs`, `src/url.rs`, `src/toml_simple.rs`,
147	  `src/logging.rs` — jj/repo helpers, URL parsing, a minimal
148	  TOML reader, the CLI logger.
149	- `src/test_helpers.rs`, `src/test_tmp_root.rs` —
150	  `#[cfg(test)]` fixtures (`Fixture` / `FixturePor`) and
151	  tempdir resolution. Subcommand modules also carry their
152	  own `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (and `integration_tests` for
153	  `sync` / `push`).
154	
155	## Subcommand model
156	
157	Adding a subcommand `x`:
158	
159	1. Create `src/x.rs` with `pub struct XArgs`
160	   (`#[derive(Args)]`), composing leaves from
161	   `src/options_flags/` where one exists.
162	2. Add a variant `X(x::XArgs)` to `Commands` in
163	   `src/main.rs` with a `///` summary (and
164	   `#[command(long_about = …)]` for the long form).
165	3. Add a dispatch arm in `main()` calling `x::x(&args)` (or,
166	   once ported, `x::x(&ctx, &params)`).
167	4. Doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive `--help`; add
168	   `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, …)]` on any field whose doc
169	   comment uses bullets (clap otherwise reflows them into
170	   prose).
171	5. Dynamic value completion (`--account=<TAB>`) is an
172	   `ArgValueCompleter` attached to the leaf in
173	   `src/options_flags/` — completion is a clap-aware concern
174	   and stays at this layer.
175	
176	
177	## Migration A — args → Context + Params
178	
179	Port each subcommand's `pub fn x(args: &XArgs)` to
180	`pub fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XParams)`, adding an
181	`XParams` flat struct + `impl From<&XArgs> for XParams` at
182	the binary edge. `init` was done as the worked example in
183	0.44.0; the rest follow the same shape. The live checklist
184	is the "Subcommand layer / CLI decoupling" item in
185	[`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md).
186	
187	| Subcommand | Status |
188	| --- | --- |
189	| `init` | done (0.44.0) — `init(&Context, &InitParams)` |
190	| `finalize` | partial — `FinalizeArgs::into_opts(log)` → `FinalizeOpts` (no `Context`; `into_opts` rather than `From`) |
191	| `sync` | not started |
192	| `chid` | not started |
193	| `desc` | not started |
194	| `list` | not started |
195	| `show` | not started |
196	| `validate-desc` | not started |
197	| `fix-desc` | not started |
198	| `clone` | not started |
199	| `push` | not started |
200	| `symlink` | not started |
201	
202	Out of scope for the early ports (deferred until a real
203	consumer surfaces): typed errors, returned outcomes vs
204	`println!`, the `ProgressSink`, and any `Context` fields
205	beyond workspace root + `UserConfig`.
206	
207	## Migration B — per-subcommand flags → `src/options_flags/`
208	
209	Independently of Migration A, per-subcommand inline `#[arg]`
210	fields are being lifted into reusable leaves/bundles under
211	`src/options_flags/` so a flag is defined (parser, help,
212	completer) exactly once. See
213	[`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md)
214	for the leaf / bundle / Pattern-A mechanics and the
215	Flag-vs-Option classification.
216	
217	State today:
218	
219	- **`init`** — fully composed from leaves/bundles
220	  (`account`, `repo`, `scope`, the `provision` bundle,
221	  `use_template`, `config`).
222	- **`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`** — share
223	  `common::CommonArgs`, a per-domain shared struct rather
224	  than an `options_flags/` leaf. The "CommonArgs sweep"
225	  todo item folds these into the leaf model (and drops the
226	  repeatable `-R`/`--repo` in favor of the `--scope` path
227	  form).
228	- **`sync` / `push` / `clone` / `validate-desc` /
229	  `fix-desc` / `finalize` / `symlink`** — still mostly
230	  inline `#[arg]` fields. The `--scope` retrofits queued in
231	  [`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md) are the usual entry
232	  point for converting one.
233	
234	## See also
235	
236	- [`README.md`](README.md) — user-facing overview and
237	  per-subcommand usage.
238	- [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) — bot workflow, versioning,
239	  commit/push conventions, code conventions.
240	- [`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md)
241	  — leaf/bundle patterns for Migration B.
242	- [`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md) — live task list,
243	  including both migrations.
244	- [`notes/chores-09.md`](notes/chores-09.md) — the
245	  0.43.0 / 0.44.0 design capture this document supersedes.
```
